# Feature matrix

Status values: Not Investigated · Documented · Implemented · Partially Compatible ·
Verified Compatible · Blocked · Planned (beyond PE-DESIGN).

"Planned (beyond PE-DESIGN)" rows are features PE-DESIGN 11 does not have; they come from
[research/competitive-features.md](research/competitive-features.md) and land only as new
attributes or commands that are off unless chosen, so compatibility is unaffected.

"Documented" rows come from Brother's public online user's guide; headings and links are in
[research/pedesign11-manual-toc.md](research/pedesign11-manual-toc.md). When a Documented
feature is implemented, its row changes status instead of a second row being added: the
README's "Not yet implemented" list is generated from the Documented rows
(`tools/readme_status.py`). "Verified Compatible" requires a reproducible test against PE-DESIGN 11 reference
output made in the owner's licensed PE-DESIGN 11.50 (black box; see
[COMPATIBILITY_STATUS.md](COMPATIBILITY_STATUS.md) for the rows at the reference tier). "Oracle-tested" means checked against pyembroidery, an independent
implementation: evidence that the code works, not proof of PE-DESIGN equivalence.

The compatibility tier each row has reached in the harness (internal regression, oracle,
PE-DESIGN reference, sew-out) is generated from the compatibility report into
[COMPATIBILITY_STATUS.md](COMPATIBILITY_STATUS.md). Do not edit that table by hand.

| Area | Feature | Status | Evidence / test | Remaining |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Number of Colors) on a working copy that keeps the image's own colours (`Raster::fit_mode`); colours sewn brightest R + G + B first, each colour's regions in scan order, reversed for the 1st, 3rd, … colour, as PE sews them; | Beyond PE-DESIGN | Auto Punch subject isolation (#127's optional graph-cut foreground step), OpenStitch mode only, opt-in: `isolateSubject: true` finds the subject by an iterated graph cut after GrabCut (Rother, Kolmogorov and Blake 2004): a 5-component colour Gaussian mixture for each side (Orchard–Bouman splitting), a min-cut on the 8-connected pixel grid by Boykov–Kolmogorov max-flow in integer capacities, at most 8 rounds; the image border (a band 2 % of the shorter side) or `subjectRect` [left, top, right, bottom] in fractions of the image is the initial background, and everything outside the subject is left out whatever its colour, in place of the border-colour rule; own code from the papers, deterministic (`fmath::Det`, a pinned mask hash); the image dialog's "Isolate the subject"; hand-marked strokes (#350): `subjectStrokes` and `backgroundStrokes` (`[{points: [[x, y], …], width}]` in fractions of the image) fix the pixels they cover to subject or background, subject winning where they overlap, and the dialog's "Mark subject…" brushes them on the image (green subject, red background, brush width, Undo stroke, Clear); a cut that finds no subject is an error naming the cause rather than the whole picture sewn (#355); on p07 and d01 the bowl, the table's colour, is 2–3 % sewn by the cut alone, 55 / 63 % with one stroke along it and 98 / 99 % with a second along its rim, the table 0 / 5 % sewn (likeness 45.6 → 51.1 and 44.3 → 51.7); measured on five photo-like images (flat render, OpenStitch mode, against the whole image, background included): dog p05 69.7 → 44.6, fruit p07 68.4 → 45.6 and d01 66.6 → 44.3 (the subject cut out cleanly, but the wooden bowl goes with the table of the same colours), storybook p03 67.6 → 47.7 (a full scene with no separate background: the cut takes parts of it), lemons g06 81.3 → 81.8 (white background); | Beyond PE-DESIGN | Photo Stitch 1's fine rows in OpenStitch mode (B16.1 T2, T3, T10, T9, image-similarity plan): in colour the image is sampled every 0.25 mm along each 0.25 mm fill row instead of one colour per 3 mm Run Pitch; each row's threads are chosen by dynamic programming (squared CIELAB error weighted by a saliency map, plus a penalty per change of thread, runs at least 1 mm, 7/16 of the error carried along the row and the rest to the next row ¼ ½ ¼, a later-sewn neighbour row's thread overlapping the band's edge counted); the penalty is tuned per 2.5 mm tile to the owner's needle caps, counted on the sewn stitches with jump tie-ins and travel: at most 2.1 per mm² over the design and 3.0 in any 10 × 10 mm window (settings `densityMean`, `densityWindow`); a stitch ends where the thread changes, runs split into equal stitches of at most the Run Pitch with the needle points shifted by row (#468, 2026-11-05: a run whose length is a whole number of Run Pitches lets its first or last stitch reach up to a sixth of a stitch past the pitch so its needle points still shift; the density fit counts the unshifted points, so the rows and stitch counts are unchanged; the share of needle points on the 30 most used columns falls 28–47 % on the nine cases at Standard and High, the sunset 0.215 → 0.136); a thread's runs are sewn nearest first with travel of up to 6 mm where later layers cover it and, with `travel: fewerTrims` (the default; the user's choice, owner), also travel hidden along a path under later layers to the thread's next run (a breadth-first search over the cells it may cross unseen, sewn as straight travel stitches of at most 6 mm, issue #298: trims per 1,000 stitches 68 → 25 on the eight photos at 90 mm, the sunset 435 → 143, for about 3 points of likeness, 72.3 → 69.3) and short visible travel up to 2 mm, else a trimmed jump (`allTrims`: straight hidden travel only, unchanged, 74.0); run ends on whole 0.1 mm units so no stitch, compiled, is under 1 mm; then a local search (`optimise.rs`, direct binary search) moves run boundaries and recolours runs wherever that brings the design closer to the image as the eye blurs it at 40 cm (S-CIELAB's opponent channels, narrow Gaussians on the cells and wide ones on a 1 mm grid, weighted by CIELAB's lightness slope), never adding a needle point to a row, three fixed passes; the rows are first fitted to a 15 % (then 7 %) larger budget that the search's merges bring back under the caps, kept only if both caps hold. On the nine Photo Stitch 1 photos (`tools/image_similarity.py`, flat render) the mean score rises 55.8 → 73.6 with `allTrims` and 71.9 with `fewerTrims` (PE-DESIGN-compatible mode 58.7), the portrait 54.3 → 71.6, the sunset 60.4 → 79.4, the high-key photo 62.0 → 78.0; design means 1.6–2.0 needle points/mm², densest windows 2.2–2.9; 12–40 s per image (the local search; under 1 s without it); the boosted and unboosted attempts run at once on their own threads, the first in order that holds the caps kept, so the result is the one trying them in turn gives (#433: High on the issue's stripes 899 → 41 s, Standard 258 → 24 s on a loaded machine, byte-identical output) | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN, internal tier) | `digitize.photoStitch {style: fill, compatMode: openStitch, travel, densityMean, densityWindow}`; | Beyond PE-DESIGN | Arched text with several lines as concentric rings around one centre (#456): `text.add` / `object.setText` `arc` (mm, the first line's baseline radius, 5-1000 either way; positive arches up over the top, negative arches down under the bottom, read upright; `null` straight). Every line is a ring round the same centre, each further line one line spacing (the text's % and mm) inward arching up, outward arching down; letters turn with the ring and are cut into strokes upright (#444). A ring too tight for its line (longer than its circumference, or smaller than the capitals) is refused naming the first radius needed. An arc replaces a Text Transform preset or free envelope and the other way round; text on an outline and vertical text take none. Text Attributes pane: Arc (on/off, radius, Up/Down); on the canvas the selected arched text shows its first ring and a centre handle: dragging the centre tightens or opens the ring, past the text it arches the other way (one `object.setText`, one undo step per drag); PES 0110 export refuses it (`text.toShapes` first) | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN) | design `rings_are_one_line_spacing_apart` (radii exact to 0.01 mm both ways), `letters_turn_with_the_ring_and_read_upright_both_ways`, `too_tight_a_ring_names_the_radius_needed`, `no_arc_lays_out_as_before`; engine `a_three_line_badge_arches_and_straightens_again` (one undo step), `too_tight_or_wrong_arcs_are_refused_with_the_radius_needed`; app `arc_row_sends_radius_and_direction`, `dragging_the_centre_sets_the_radius_and_side`; concept from openhoop (GPL-3.0), no code | Spacing arrows on the canvas; the radius live while dragging (the ring shows it, the letters follow on release) |

| Beyond PE-DESIGN | AI assistant panel (B17, phase A1): a chat beside the design (View ▸ Show/Hide ▸ Assistant, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+A). Claude API with the user's own key in the OS keychain (default `claude-opus-5-5`, streaming, adaptive thinking). The model drives the editor through the A0 tools in-process. A permission gate judges each command by its effect: files, machine transfer, Exit and undoing earlier work ask (Allow once / Always allow / Deny, declined after 4 minutes), and there is an optional change limit. A bare file name of a file in the scratch folder (`import.svg {"path": "heart.svg"}` right after `scratch_write heart.svg`) is that file's absolute path for the gate and the command, so it no longer asks or looks in the working folder; names with a folder, `..`, hidden files and links out of the folder still ask (#431). Everything one message changes is one undo step, Save as script writes it out, and messages typed during a turn are queued. Preferences: Options ▸ Show AI features (on by default, owner 2026-11-04) and the panel's settings. The panel opens on first start and then as the user left it (`panelOpen` in ui.json; an older ui.json reads as open), and closing it says how to reopen it (View ▸ Show/Hide ▸ Assistant, the shortcut, and Options ▸ Show AI features when AI is off) until "Don't show this again" (#422) | Planned (beyond PE-DESIGN) | `assistant_panel::tests` (7, scripted SSE turns: one undo per message, approval/denial/timeout, Stop, key refused for plain http, Save as script rebuilds the design, queue); `editor::assistant_chat::tests` (gate, prefs, script; `a_bare_name_of_a_scratch_file_becomes_its_path`, `a_bare_name_that_links_out_of_the_scratch_folder_still_asks` (#431)); `a_scratch_file_imported_by_its_bare_name_runs_without_a_question` (#431, a scripted turn); `openstitch-assistant` (25 tests: request shape, SSE, tool loop, cut-off and invalid tool inputs not run, refusal, cancel, retry); `openstitch-app` `tests/keychain.rs` (a dummy key saved, read back, replaced and removed in the real system keychain, under a throwaway service; run by CI with `--ignored` on macOS, Windows and Linux with gnome-keyring, #401) | Landed 2026-11-04 (#306); not yet driven by the real API: the first real run (2026-11-04, A5) had no key. Keychain round trip passed on Windows (Credential Manager) 2026-11-05 (#401). Readability (#581, 2026-11-06): the panel's notices are at the normal size in the theme's text colour, problems (an agent that did not start, errors, a key not sent) in its error colour, no italics; the model list opens from the app's own drop-down icon; a saved key shows as `**** saved`; `problems_stand_out_and_information_does_not`, screenshots `rust-editor-assistant-notices-light.png` / `-dark.png` (tools/screenshots.py) |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | AI assistant providers (B17, phases A2-A3). A2: besides the Claude API, any OpenAI-compatible `/chat/completions` endpoint, with presets for Ollama and LM Studio on this computer (no key needed) and OpenRouter. The settings choose the protocol, list the endpoint's models, set the request timeout (30-3600 s) and an optional reasoning effort, and warn when an Ollama model has no `tools` capability. Choosing the Ollama or LM Studio preset drops the Claude model and picks the first model the server lists that can call tools (Ollama's `/api/show` capabilities; a server that is not Ollama gives its first model); with no model a message says to choose one instead of failing with a 404, and an empty model is kept across restarts (#432). A notice shows when a model answers an edit request without using its tools. A3: coding agents over the Agent Client Protocol (Claude Code via claude-agent-acp, Codex via codex-acp, OpenCode and others) on the user's own account. They are found on PATH and in the usual install folders, with install commands for missing ones. Each runs in its own process group in an empty folder, with no file system or terminal declared and Claude Code's own shell, file and web tools turned off. Its only way into the editor is `openstitch-mcp --assistant` on a private loopback socket whose every request passes the panel's gate | Planned (beyond PE-DESIGN) | `openstitch-assistant` `tests/openai.rs` (24: streamed tool calls across chunks, a tool call Ollama lost mid-stream asked again and a stream that just stops not retried (#429), an empty reply with output tokens reported (#425), a text-only model's pictures as notes after a 400 (#416), an unreadable tool call asked again (#427), a 400 without pictures not retried, parallel calls, invalid or cut-off calls not run, refusal, cancel, no key for loopback only, no Authorization header without a key, body shape, model lists for both protocols, the Ollama capability, the protocol lock); `tests/local_preset.rs` (4, #432: a mock Ollama and a mock LM Studio on a real 127.0.0.1 socket: the first model with tools, none with tools, LM Studio's first model, an empty list or no server); `a_local_preset_does_not_keep_the_claude_model`, `a_local_preset_picks_a_model_with_tools_for_the_first_message` (mock Ollama), `an_empty_model_asks_for_one_instead_of_sending`; `openstitch-mcp/tests/assistant.rs` (no meta tool has a `name` argument, the old `name` still accepted, #425); `assistant_panel::tests` (a coding agent is offered no API key (#420), a local model with no key, one undo step, the talk-only notice, a remote endpoint refused without a key, a protocol change starting a new conversation, a coding agent's socket going through the gate); `tests/acp.rs` (10, a scripted ACP agent: updates mapped to events, the bridge tool allowed and a foreign one refused, `fs/*` refused, `initialize` and `session/new` contents including `settingSources: []` (#390), sign-in required, cancel, discovery, process-group stop) | A2 landed 2026-11-04 (#317); A3 landed 2026-11-04 (#338). | Beyond PE-DESIGN | AI assistant benchmark (B17, phase A5): `stitchtool bench-assistant` sends 12 embroidery tasks (badge, monogram, arc text, appliqué, star, two colours, a wider rectangle, recolour, delete, picture to embroidery, vector route, a question) to the Claude API, an OpenAI-compatible endpoint or an ACP coding agent, through the assistant's own tools over a headless editor. Each task is scored on the resulting design (`object.list`, `design.info`), failures are sorted into categories, and it writes JSON and Markdown reports. Since #493: 23 tasks. Six illustrated subjects (strawberry, mushroom with three panels, watermelon with a satin rind, lightbulb with a face, croissant with satin outlines, cat face) are scored on structure: parts, size, colours, touching fills at least 20° apart, and `design.check` without errors. An Auto Punch task on a Higgsfield emblem must read the plan (`dryRun`), override a region and leave the white background unsewn. A 5 mm bean-stitch name must use a font whose `sizeRange` holds the size. `--judge MODEL` has a vision model score each render 1-5 (recognisable, parts, colours) on a fixed rubric, and the reports carry the uncut tool calls | Planned (beyond PE-DESIGN) | `stitchtool` `bench::tests` (scripted Claude endpoint, no network: a pass, an "only talked", an answered question; failure categories; every task's setup runs; `the_illustrated_tasks_score_structure`, `the_auto_punch_and_small_name_tasks` (each rule on hand-made passing and failing designs), `the_judge_scores_are_read_from_its_reply`, `the_judge_scores_the_render_of_each_task` (a scripted judge's answer is the score)) | Done 2026-11-04 (#365). Real run 2026-11-04 with Claude Code (claude-agent-acp 0.100.0, `claude-opus-5-5`): 12 of 12 tasks passed, 113 tool calls (2 errors), 5.72 M input / 16.7 k output tokens; 3.61 M after #390. Ollama runs 2026-11-04: gpt-oss:20b 8 of 12 (7 of 12 with the network off), qwen3:14b 3 of 12. The Claude API and an image generator were not run (no key, no generator). 2026-11-06 (#492, #493), Windows, Claude Code over claude-agent-acp: **22 of 23** in 1,218 s, 265 tool calls (7 errors), 10.4 M input / 55.6 k output tokens. The 8 new tasks passed 7 of 8 (115 calls, 4.76 M / 28.6 k tokens, 524 s); every illustrated task opened its example by name. `small-name` failed: in three runs out of three Claude chose `openstitch-line` at 5 mm in bean stitch, then gave up on live text because the letters are joined by sewn travel (#548). `--judge` was not run (it needs an API key) |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | AI assistant memory between conversations (#491): the Assistant keeps short notes about the user (Machine, Threads, Fabric, Preferences, Lessons) in `assistant-notes.md` beside `ui.json`, at most 8 KB, and reads them at the start of every conversation in its system prompt, for the Claude API, OpenAI-compatible endpoints and coding agents. It writes with `notes_write` (append a line or replace a section; `ui.notesWrite`), always behind the permission gate ("remember (Threads): ..."; Allow once / Always allow / Deny); a denied write changes nothing. Over the cap a write is refused with a request to summarise; text that looks like a password, key or token is refused. Assistant settings: "Let the Assistant remember things" (on by default; `ui.set assistantMemory`, which also asks), the notes as editable text with their size, Save notes, Undo edits and Forget everything (`ui.notesClear`). Memory off: no notes in the prompt and the tools refused without asking. The notes go only to the provider the user chose, never into a design or an export. Skill: when to write (lasting facts, corrections that worked) and never secrets | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN, internal tier) | `openstitch-assistant` `notes::tests` (6: append and replace, sections, secrets and unknown sections refused, the cap, the prompt block, the file); `assistant_panel::tests`: `a_fact_remembered_in_one_conversation_is_in_the_next_ones_system_prompt` (the gate question, the file, a new conversation in a restarted editor), `denied_writes_and_memory_off_change_nothing`, `the_notes_reach_every_protocol`, `notes_through_ui_commands_and_in_the_settings`; `editor::assistant_chat::tests::memory_changes_always_ask`; `openstitch-mcp/tests/assistant.rs` `notes_tools_forward_to_the_editor`; `stitchtool` bench pair `remember` then `remembered-heart` (passes when the heart is a red Madeira Polyneon thread), `the_memory_pair_carries_the_notes_into_the_next_conversation` | Landed 2026-11-06 (#491). | Beyond PE-DESIGN | More fabric presets, thread weight driving density and stabiliser/needle/minimum-text advice, named machine profiles and a first-run machine setup (#508). Part 1 landed: five more fabrics after PE-DESIGN's eight (polyester woven, piqué knit, organza and sheers, canvas, structured caps; `beyondPeDesign` in `fabric.list`), and for every fabric and thread weight (No. 12, 30, 40, 60) the backing, topping, needle point and size and the smallest legible block-letter height, from the stabiliser, needle and thread makers' published guidance with each source listed and makers' disagreements named (`fabric.advise {fabric, weight}`; `crates/openstitch-engine/data/fabric_advice.json`; [research/beyond/fabric-presets.md](research/beyond/fabric-presets.md)); the Fabric Selector shows backing, topping, needle and smallest text. Part 2: thread weight drives density (`fabric.apply {fabric, weight}`: fill and satin rows per mm × √(weight / 40), so No. 60 sews rows 0.82× as far apart; No. 40 leaves the values unchanged), and `fabric.setDefault {fabric, weight}` (user settings; `options.get` `defaultFabric`) gives shapes and texts drawn afterwards the fabric's settings in the same undo step; the Fabric Selector has a thread weight choice, shows the density and advice for it, and a "New shapes and texts start from this fabric" check box. Part 3: the user's machine (`machine.setProfile {brand, hoop, cutter, maxStitchMm}`, `machine.profile` with the brand's own file format; user settings): `design.new` without a size starts on its hoop, and every export warns about stitches longer than the machine sews and about trims a machine without a cutter will not cut (unless Stop at Jumps is on), the file itself unchanged; Help ▸ Machine Setup, shown once on the first interactive start | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN) | engine `thread_weight_scales_the_fabric_density`, `new_shapes_start_from_the_default_fabric`, `a_profile_starts_new_designs_on_its_hoop_and_persists`, `exports_warn_for_the_users_machine_and_write_the_same_bytes`, `every_brand_has_a_writable_format`; app `the_first_interactive_start_asks_once_and_help_asks_again`; `thirteen_fabrics_and_pe_designs_eight_first`, `the_advice_table_covers_every_fabric_and_cites_known_sources`, `advise_gives_every_fabric_and_weight_with_its_sources`, `a_custom_fabric_is_advised_as_its_base_and_new_fabrics_apply`; [research/competitive-features.md](research/competitive-features.md) Updates, 2026-11-05 full survey (filum (GPL-3.0), openhoop (GPL-3.0), Roidery (Apache-2.0), threadspool (no licence); concepts only) | Profiles hold only what the user states: no per-model limits are built in (makers publish them per model, in each manual); one sew-out per new fabric waits for the owner. The weight rule is a starting point (thread makers publish spacing per weight, not per fabric); the app's own New Objects defaults (#553), when set, are applied after the fabric's. No maker publishes density or pull compensation per fabric, so the five new fabrics' numbers are OpenStitch's approximations like the eight; minimum text for No. 30 and No. 12 is an estimate (stated) |

| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Knockdown (laydown) fill sewn first under the design or selection, gaps between nearby shapes closed, one or two crossed layers (B12): `wizard.knockdown {ids?, reach, gapClose, spacing, stitch, angle, layers, color, shape: outline\|rectangle, keepHoles}` makes an editable Fill named Knockdown (no underlay) before the first covered object, `{update: id}` rebuilds it, `order.optimize` keeps it before what it covers; Sewing Order ▸ Knockdown / Update knockdown (#510) | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN) | engine `knockdown.rs`: `knockdown_covers_the_union_and_stays_first`, `knockdown_keeps_large_holes`; [research/competitive-features.md](research/competitive-features.md) Updates, 2026-11-05 full survey (openhoop (GPL-3.0), filum (GPL-3.0); concepts only) | Planned feature, P2 |

| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Free multi-hooping (#515, W1): Design Settings ▸ Split design ▸ Hoopings Free (Grid, PE-DESIGN's, stays the default): hoopings of the section hoop placed anywhere and overlapping, in sewing order, each optionally turned 90°; each visible object (a group as one) goes whole to the first hooping that holds it, so no letter, group or satin is cut; objects that fit no hooping are outlined red, listed in `sections.list` `errors` and block `sections.export` with their name. The alignment mark (boxed X, cross or none) sits in the middle of the overlap of consecutive hoopings: sewn at the end of the earlier one and at the start of the later one, there in the hooping's first thread and followed by a stop (no extra colour block); consecutive hoopings must overlap at least the minimum (16 mm default). Add left / right / up / down, Remove, positions in mm, Auto layout (`sections.autoLayout`: the fewest hoopings in either orientation covering the design with the overlap). | Beyond PE-DESIGN | View toggles (#517), View ▸ Show (a menu, reusing the view's eye icon): Needle Points (a dot at every needle drop), Underlay (both, underlay only, top only), Trim Marks (a cut mark at every trim of the compiled pattern; trimmed moves dashed red, untrimmed jumps grey), Hide Unselected (only the selection's stitches), Colour by (thread, sewing order: a fixed 16-step gradient from the first stitch to the last, monochrome) and Solo Colour (every block but the simulator's, else the selection's, faded to 20 % over the page); all off by default, kept in `ui.json`. Underlay is told from top stitching by compiling the design once more with every object's underlay off: a needle point that compile does not sew (matched from the end) is underlay. `openstitch_design::view` turns the compiled pattern into a drawing-only pattern, so the design and its export never change; the editor's canvas, the editor's `ui.render` (what `view_render` shows: View ▸ Show's settings, each overridable) and MCP `preview` share it, with `layers`, `colorBy`, `only`, `solo`, `needlePoints` and `trimMarks` (needle points and trim marks drawn by `openstitch_engine::preview::png_shown`); the Assistant sets each with `ui.set` (`needlePoints`, `showLayers`, `trimMarks`, `hideUnselected`, `colorBy`, `soloColor`) | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN, internal tier; off unless chosen) | design `view.rs`: `underlay_only_shows_exactly_the_underlay_and_top_only_the_rest`, `hide_unselected_colour_by_order_monochrome_and_solo`, `trim_marks_are_at_every_trim_and_nowhere_else`; app `prefs.rs` (saved, off by default); `assistant_ui.rs` `settings_read_and_write_every_entry` ("show me only the underlay"); mcp `capabilities.rs` (preview flags), `assistant.rs` `view_render_returns_an_image_block_and_the_stats` (overrides forwarded); app `assistant_ui.rs` `ui_render_shows_what_view_show_sets` (top only, hide unselected, solo colour, needle points, trim marks and monochrome each change the picture) | Solo colour fades by blending into the page colour in the Stitch view; the Realistic and Solid views draw the filtered stitches but not the fade's exact look; scripted screenshots per mode |

| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Ripple line (#541): `object.setLine {type: ripple, strands (copies 2-30), target [x, y] mm, spacing, pitch}`. A closed line spirals in to the target as one unbroken run (the copy's scale falls evenly along each turn, ending at the target: no jump, no trim); an open line is sewn back and forth beside itself, each copy `spacing` further towards the target's side. The target defaults to the line's centre, follows moves of the object, and is dragged on the canvas (a ring-and-cross marker on the selected ripple line; one `object.setLine`, one undo step per drag). Inspector: copies, spacing, pitch; PES 0110 export refuses it | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN) | design `a_closed_line_spirals_in_to_the_target_as_one_run`, `an_open_line_repeats_back_and_forth_spacing_apart` (spacing ±10 %); engine `a_closed_ripple_spirals_in_without_trims_and_its_target_moves`; app `a_target_drag_is_one_set_line_in_millimetres`; every line type through `every_line_type_is_set_sewn_saved_and_undone`; concepts from openhoop and filum (GPL-3.0), no code | Taper and a spacing step on closed lines |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Back stitch and a running stitch along a thickness profile (#542): line type `backStitch` (two stitches forward, one back, each back stitch half the forward one, ending at the far end); on running lines `object.setLine {profile: constant\|linear\|arc\|sine\|smoothstep\|stairs, profileSteps, profilePoints [[t, v]], minPasses, maxPasses (odd), passSpacing}` sews each stitch back and forth the profile's number of times, passes side by side, so the line swells like a brush stroke and still ends at its far end. The profile type (`line_looks::Profile`, presets and custom points) is shared for B6 and B10 later. Inspector: Back stitch type, Thickness profile on running lines | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN) | design `presets_have_their_documented_values` (values at 0, 0.5, 1), `back_stitch_goes_two_forward_one_back_and_ends_at_the_end`, `variable_running_follows_the_arc_profile` (min, midpoint and max passes; no stitch under 0.3 mm but the pass offsets); engine `back_stitch_and_a_profiled_running_line` (one undo step); concepts from openhoop (GPL-3.0) and stitch_generator (MIT), no code | Profiles on satin width (B10) and density (B6); NV880E sew-out |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Decorative hand-embroidery lines (#543): `object.setLine {type: chevron \| cretan \| feather \| overlock \| alternatingTriangles \| doubleZigzag \| lattice \| stripes, width, spacing, height, strands, gaps, pitch}` on any open or closed line, laid out along the line so they follow curves: chevron (a bar along the line at each zigzag peak), Cretan (a short stitch in from each peak), feather (the side stitches turned forward), overlock (a serger-like edge), alternating triangles (a zigzag between two outline rails), double zigzag (a zigzag and its mirror: a chain of diamonds, the return points midway between the first pass's on each side, no jump), lattice (3-7 strands crossing into a braid), stripes (2-12 rows, or rows at a list of gaps for pinstripes 1-2-1-2-1); no stitch over 7 mm, none sewn three times in place. E stitch `prongAngle` (-60...60°) slants the prongs; 0 sews PE-DESIGN's square prongs unchanged. Inspector: the eight types with band width, period, side-stitch length, strands; E stitch prong angle; gallery picture docs/images/engine-line-types-decorative.png from examples/line-types-decorative.json; PES 0110 export refuses them | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN) | design `every_pattern_sews_straight_curved_and_closed_lines_in_bounds`, `double_zigzag_returns_midway_without_a_jump`, `stripes_keep_their_count_and_gaps` (gaps ±0.2 mm), `cretan_and_feather_side_stitches_point_in`, `e_stitch_prongs_slant_by_their_angle` (45 ± 1°); engine `every_decorative_line_sews_within_seven_millimetres_and_undoes`, `stripes_lattice_and_slanted_e_stitch_take_their_settings`; every line type through `every_line_type_is_set_sewn_saved_and_undone`; concepts from stitch_generator (MIT), no code | Defaults from an NV880E sew-out (owner); stripes that follow a varying band width |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Motif line placement (#544, partial): `object.setLine` on motif lines takes `motifGroups [[count, gap mm]]` (motifs in groups with a longer gap after each, repeated; the editor's `groupSize`/`groupGap` set one), `freeStart`/`freeEnd` (mm of running stitch with no motif at the ends) and `motifs [ids]` (more motifs used in turn; `arrange: alternate` already mirrors every other one). Without them the line is PE-DESIGN's, unchanged (compat `line-motif-zigzag-3pt`). Five more bundled motifs: square spiral, star, X, zigzag, arrow (`patterns.list`), shown in docs/images/engine-motif-catalogue.png. Inspector: Motif in turn, Group size, Group gap, Free start, Free end; PES 0110 export refuses the plan. Motif band: `object.setRegion` on skeleton satin and Manual Punch satin regions and `object.setLine` on satin lines take `motifBand` (a motif id, or none), `bandTile` (each copy's length, 1-50 mm; default the mean width) and `bandMirror`: the motif repeated along the column instead of the satin, each copy stretched from edge to edge so it narrows and widens with a taper (docs/images/engine-motif-band.png); inspector Motif band, Band copy length, Mirror every other copy | Partial (beyond PE-DESIGN) | design `motif_groups_free_ends_and_motifs_in_turn` (tips 5 mm apart in a group, 12 mm between groups, none in the free ends; the plain plan equals PE-DESIGN's line), `the_motif_follows_the_local_width_of_a_tapered_column` (2 to 8 mm taper, every copy touching both edges, within 0.2 mm), `copies_fill_the_column_and_mirror_alternately`; engine `motif_groups_free_ends_and_motifs_in_turn_through_the_command`, `a_skeleton_satin_band_follows_its_taper`, `satin_lines_and_columns_take_a_band_and_others_refuse_it`; concepts from stitch_generator (MIT), no code | An image per line-type option |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Travel under later fills and per-object run connectors (B6 hidden travel, B4): `design.setTravelUnder {on}` sews a move into an object of the same thread as 2 mm running stitches when it lies inside the fills sewn from that object on, 0.5 mm in from their edges (straight or the shortest way round their corners, a visibility graph since 2026-11-07), instead of a jump and trim, or else along running and triple lines of that thread already sewn when both ends lie on them (lines that meet join; at most three times the straight way, 2026-11-07); `object.setConnector {ids, connector: jump|run}` joins an object to the next of the same thread with a visible 2 mm running connector, listing those of 15 mm or more (`longConnectors`); Sewing Order ▸ Then: run, Design Settings ▸ Production (#545) | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN; joining a run to the middle of a satin not done) | engine `routing.rs`: `travel_under_a_later_fill_replaces_the_trim`, `travel_runs_along_a_running_line_of_its_thread`, `run_connectors_join_objects_of_one_thread`; design `hidden::tests::*`; [research/competitive-features.md](research/competitive-features.md) Updates, 2026-11-05 full survey (Roidery (Apache-2.0), openhoop (GPL-3.0), filum (GPL-3.0); concepts only) | Planned feature, P2 |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Branching and auto-routing (B4): `shape.autoRoute {ids, redwork, join, keep}` makes one line per connected piece of touching or crossing running lines, sewn in one walk that doubles back only by the minimum matching of the odd vertices (route inspection, exact up to 16 odd vertices), or every line twice and back to the start (Redwork); `object.branch {ids, start}` groups touching shapes in the first one's thread, orders them nearest-next and enters each where the one before touches it (no move, no trim), re-linked after every edit; `object.unbranch`; Sewing Order ▸ Branch / Unbranch, Auto-route lines, Redwork (#546) | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN; satin branching with centre-run underlay and canvas start/end picking not done) | engine `branching.rs`: `a_tree_of_lines_sews_with_no_trims`, `redwork_sews_each_line_twice_and_threads_route_apart`, `a_branch_sews_touching_shapes_in_one_go`; design `graph::tests::*`; [research/competitive-features.md](research/competitive-features.md) Updates, 2026-11-05 full survey (openhoop (GPL-3.0), filum (GPL-3.0); concepts only) | Planned feature, P2 |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Automatic nearest start per object by stitch type and a before/after report from Optimize (B11): `design.setProperties {autoStart: nearest}` sets, at compile time, the entry point of each shape without one to the nearest point of its outline (regions, closed lines) or its nearer end (open lines); text and stitch patterns keep their own start. `order.optimize` returns `before`/`after` colour changes, jumps, trims and travel mm and a summary the editor shows in the status bar; `design.info` `stitchStats.travelMM` (#547) | Implemented (beyond PE-DESIGN; part-way satin start and draggable markers not done) | engine `production.rs`: `nearest_start_shortens_travel`, `optimize_reports_before_and_after`; measured in COMPATIBILITY_AUDIT "Production aids, 2026-11-06"; [research/competitive-features.md](research/competitive-features.md) Updates, 2026-11-05 full survey (Roidery (Apache-2.0), openhoop (GPL-3.0), filum (GPL-3.0); concepts only) | Planned feature, P2; PE-DESIGN has a partial equivalent: its part belongs in the PE-compat track |
| Beyond PE-DESIGN | Per-object finish (#548): `object.setFinish {ids, trim, trimDistance, tie, tieCount, tieLength, between, reset}`. **Trim after** the object: `auto` (PE-DESIGN: when the next start is farther than the trim distance; `trimDistance` 0-50 mm overrides the design's), `always` (however short the move), `off` (the frame jumps; a colour change still cuts). **Ties**: `auto` (PE-DESIGN's on the runs a trim separates), `always` (also at the object's start and end), `off` (none); `tieCount` 1-5 out-and-back steps of `tieLength` 0.2-2 mm replace PE-DESIGN's own tie (each step two stitches of that length, the generators' ties left out). At a pointed start, where the tie and the tip's first rows or crossings would sew more than 6 stitches in place, the tie steps out to the tip's first needle point beyond 0.35 mm instead (at most 1 mm; a tie longer than 6 stitches by itself is sewn as set; #732). **Between letters** of a text: `auto` (PE-DESIGN: moves over 2.3 mm), `never`, `letter`, `word`, `line` (letters not trimmed apart are sewn across up to 2.3 mm or jump). An object at every default stores no finish and sews as before.
